Title 30 Ch. 5 is the Protection From Abuse Act; § 30-5-7 confirmed at its number, history Acts 1981, No. 81-476; Acts 1995, No. 95-542; Act 2010-538; Act 2015-493. No address-confidentiality program ("address confidentiality" 0 units, "substitute address" 0). VAWA read whole (FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491). Rows: `edu-dv-eviction-protection-al`, `edu-dv-lockchange-al`, `edu-dv-deposit-timing-al`, `edu-dv-confidentiality-al`, `edu-dv-protection-order-law-al`, `edu-dv-qualifying-documents-al`. Finding on `edu-no-dv-termination-al` in §6.
- **Item 10 (binding-arbitration).** § 35-9A-163(b) gives damages for knowingly enforcing a lease containing a prohibited term, so AL is not tagged on `arbitration-agreement` and Taylor was not asked; `edu-binding-arbitration-al` modeled on the SC and TN rows. Title 6 Ch. 6 Art. 1 read whole, § 8-1-41(3) read, FEDERAL: 9 U.S.C. § 2 read on govinfo.
- **Item 8 (three central shared edits).**
  - `smoking-policy`: holds for Alabama. Re-run AL-M-025 over Code, Constitution and acts: cannabis or marijuana near a tenant, landlord or rental-agreement word 0 units; the AND line (cannabis, a dwelling or role word, smoke) hits only Act 2025-415, an Elmore County tobacco and vapor tax act. The medical cannabis act gives no right to smoke anywhere: 'medical cannabis' 'does not include' 'Any product administered by smoking, combustion, or vaping.' (Ala. Code § 20-2A-3(14)b.2.) No statute limits a private landlord's smoking ban. Charging only for smoking by Tenant, an occupant or a guest is a damages term for the tenant's breach and limits no landlord liability (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)).
  - `no-alterations`: holds for Alabama. § 24-8-7 read whole; the AL segment's citation of Ala. Code § 24-8-7(g)(1) (reasonable modifications at the tenant's expense, restoration 'where it is reasonable to do so', 'reasonable wear and tear excepted') matches the text. Battery AL-M-026.r2 (supersedes AL-M-026, whose 300-character windows were too short): no Alabama statute makes a tenant-installed fixture, EV charger, solar or antenna installation the residential tenant's property; the hits are goods-leasing fixtures (Ala. Code § 7-2A-309), condemnation compensation for tenant-owned improvements (Ala. Code § 18-1A-29, Ala. Code § 18-4-18), a mechanic's lien on leased land (Ala. Code § 35-11-212) and tenant upkeep duties (Ala. Code § 35-9A-301). "Trade fixtures" 0; EV charging near a role word 0.
  - `addendum-precedence`: precedence holds (the lease prevails over a rule, Ala. Code § 35-9A-302(c); no statute makes an addendum control over a lease; no form in `edu-statutory-forms-al` is an addendum). The first sentence does not hold: it makes attached addenda part of the Lease, which conflicts with the separate-document rules in Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(d)(1) and Ala. Code § 35-9A-303(b)(4), (d). Finding in §6, proposed row `addendum-precedence-al`.
- **Item 11 (agency rules and the § 35-9B-2 cross-reference).** Every agency-rule citation in the new rows was checked against the saved rule text with a script that finds the rule heading above each quotation: 22 quotations, all now under the cited rule. One fix: the pool row (`edu-no-pool-safety-rule-al`) had cited the hotel definition as r. 420-3-11-.02 and the pool paragraph as r. 420-3-11-.05 from an excerpt; Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-11 was then read in the chapter PDF (26 pages, rule headings .01 to .20) and the citations corrected to Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-11-.01(2)(o) (agency rule) and Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-11-.16(5)(a) (agency rule); rules .01 and .16 saved whole. State Fire Marshal (Ala. Admin. Code ch. 482-2-101, 482-2-103, 482-2-108, 482-2-109), Public Service Commission (ch. 770-X-1, 770-X-3), Health lead (ch. 420-3-27, 420-3-29) and Real Estate Commission (ch. 790-X-3) rules are each flagged as agency rules in the rows. § 35-9B-2(a)(4) still refers to "Section 34-9A-441", which does not exist; reported in §7.

#### Per-topic decisions

- binding-arbitration: Alabama item 10: § 35-9A-163(b) gives damages for knowingly enforcing a lease with a prohibited term, so `arbitration-agreement` is not tagged AL; education row written instead (prompt: do not ask Taylor).
- casualty-and-mitigation-waivable: casualty-and-mitigation-waivable: waiver of § 35-9A-406 or § 35-9A-105(a) is not on § 35-9A-163(a)'s list but enforceability is case law; library declines both clauses (rule 54 education row).
- confession-of-judgment: Ala. Code § 8-9-11 also voids pre-suit agreements to be sued in another county; recorded on this row and to be used by any venue topic.
- deposit-escheat: Alabama item 4: § 35-9A-201(d) 90-day forfeiture vs the unclaimed property act (§ 35-12-71(16) lists "security deposit"; § 35-12-72(a)(19) three years). Unsettled; cautious course is to hold and report at three years, since the reporting penalty is the costlier error.
- designated-repairer: designated-repairer: optional clause offered (rule 54); preserves the § 35-9A-401 and § 35-9A-404 remedies; reuses {{landlord_repair_contact}}.
- forfeiture-redemption: Alabama item 9(a): `early-termination-ks` fee fails the works-under-every-reading test; proposed `early-termination-al` (flat one month).
- forfeiture-redemption: Alabama item 9(b): `holdover-rate-al` fails rule 53 (stacking with § 35-9A-441(c), pay-to-stay on appeal, same-day rent); replacement `holdover-rate-al-v2` proposed.
- forfeiture-redemption: Alabama item 9(c): AL eviction rows checked against FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c); finding on `edu-eviction-process-al`.
- jury-waiver: jury-waiver: lawful status unknown (case law); library declines a clause and says so in an education row (rule 54).
- landlord-registration: Alabama item 12: landlord-registration answered with all four checks; the foreign-entity bar on maintaining suits (Ala. Code § 10A-1-7.21(a)) is a precondition to an eviction filing for unregistered foreign entities.
- nonrefundable-deposit-notice: nonrefundable-deposit-notice: tag `nonrefundable-fees-wv` (true for AL as written; it labels fees and routes any security into the deposit).
- waiver-by-acceptance: waiver-by-acceptance: § 35-9A-424 lets only an agreement made after the breach prevent waiver, so a lease non-waiver clause is no answer; education row explains the post-breach agreement.
- alarm-tampering-fee: Verdict 'Confirmed absent' (the AL row is a confirmed-absence row on the fee); the tag of the optional clause rides with it.
- alarm-tampering-fee: Rule 54: Alabama law lets the lease restate the tenant's damage duty and recover actual cost; offered through the tag on `detector-care-la` rather than a new AL clause. A fixed fee is not offered (log §4 class decision 6).
- appliances-excluded: Tag of appliances-excluded-la: keyed to this topic, health-and-safety limit built in, so true for Alabama as written (log §4 class decisions 1, 2, 4 checked).
- appliances-excluded: After the independent check (round 1): AL not tagged on `appliances-excluded-la` (class decision 2); its last sentence ("it does not limit Landlord's liability for causing physical injury or for intentional or gross fault") can be read to limit liability for ordinary negligence, which Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4) bars. Alabama clause `appliances-excluded-al` written with "it does not limit any liability Landlord has under law".
- bed-bug-cooperation: Tagged pest-cooperation-pr rather than pest-cooperation-la: the pr version names household members, matching Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(f), and defers entry to the lease's entry section.
- carpet-cleaning: `carpet-cleaning-ar` not tagged AL: it applies the cleaning cost to the deposit 'as damages caused by Tenant's noncompliance with this Lease', but Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(b) lets the deposit cover only accrued rent and damages from noncompliance with Ala. Code § 35-9A-301; a lease-only duty does not fit unless the soiling breaches § 35-9A-301(2) or (6). Rule 54 option (a professional-cleaning clause) declined for Alabama; education row says so.
- condemned-premises-rent-bar: Cautious course chosen for rent during an occupancy ban (case law only could settle it); row stays VERIFIED (Part 5).
- environmental-event-termination: Rule 54 option found (opt-in environmental-event termination) and declined: risk of operating as a waiver of repair rights under Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(1); education row says so.
- fire-code-standard: Education only: no lease term is needed; code compliance is a landlord duty under Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(a)(1) and a duty on every owner under Ala. Code § 36-19-11 (rule 55).
- fire-code-standard: Section-wide AND lines use ^ (string start, no m flag) because JavaScript has no \A; same as S2's note in AL-S2-001.
- fire-code-standard: Kept VERIFIED: the Fire Marshal rule was read; the Alabama Residential Building Code's IRC edition is not located and the row says so without stating an edition (fetch request filed).
- fire-code-standard: Ala. Admin. Code r. 465-X-1-.01(17) cited in notes as the Board's by-reference definition; not quoted (chapter scanned for terms, not saved).
- fire-code-standard: Fetch requests closed by the main pass: the row names no residential code edition and quotes no International Fire Code text; it rests on Ala. Admin. Code r. 482-2-101-.02 (agency rule) and the statutes. ICC and Home Builders Licensure Board publications are outside this pass's sources.
- fire-sprinkler-duty: Present, not Confirmed absent: Alabama has statutes on point (bans on residential sprinkler mandates); no landlord retrofit duty found.
- fire-sprinkler-duty: effective_from 2024-10-01: Ala. Code § 34-14A-12.2(a) (Act 2024-443) controls for buildings of up to four units.
- fire-sprinkler-duty: Fetch request closed by the main pass: the ICC code text is outside this pass's sources; the row does not describe Appendix M's content beyond what the Fire Marshal rule says and gives the cautious course (ask the State Fire Marshal), and the question to Taylor records the conflict.
- habitability-materiality: Education only: the materiality tests are statutory and cannot be set by lease (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(1)).
- heating: No new heat clause (log §4 class decision 1): SFR heat shift already covered by `tenant-repair-agreement-al` (checkbox for supplying heat); non-SFR only by that separate writing. `tenant-supplied-heat-wy` not tagged (Wyoming-specific citations).
- lead-safe-certification: Confirmed absent for the certificate; the certification-of-people rules are stated as what applies instead.
- lead-safe-certification: Statute/rule tension on the owner exemption (Ala. Code § 22-37A-5(b)(1) vs Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-29-.02(6)) handled by the cautious course, VERIFIED (Part 5: only case law could settle it).
- lead-state-notices: effective_from 2022-03-17: current version of Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-29-.04 (amended effective March 17, 2022).
- lead-state-notices: Education only (rule 55): the pamphlet duty sits on the renovating firm; no lease term is needed.
- pest-control-notice: Fetch request closed by the main pass: Ala. Admin. Code ch. 80-10-9 (structural pest control, 32 pages, rules .01 to .28) read whole in tab-1 on 2026-11-08 and searched for notification, occupant, tenant and posting provisions: none requires notice to occupants before treatment (the notification rules run between the permittee and the Commissioner). The absence row stands.
- pool-safety: Rule numbers .02 and .05 for the hotel-rule excerpts inferred from the chapter's structure (definition list and pool paragraph); the saved excerpt carries the text. If the main pass needs the exact rule number, read the TOC of 420-3-11.
- repair-cost-termination: Rule 54: the only lawful landlord-serving option here (a casualty termination clause) already exists as `casualty-landlord-termination-al`; no new clause.
- security-devices: `security-devices-wy` not tagged: it lets the landlord deduct rekey costs from the deposit 'as a cost provided by this Lease', but Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(b) applies a deposit only to accrued rent and damages from the tenant's noncompliance with Ala. Code § 35-9A-301; whether a lease-created charge counts as rent under Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12) is case law. No AL clause offered.
- smart-access: `smart-access-system-wy` not tagged: it adds landlord duties (privacy notice, non-app credential) beyond Alabama law and uses Wyoming-pass variables; offered nowhere for AL; education only.
- sprinkler-disclosure: Education only; an optional sprinkler description in the lease is not offered as a clause because it is neither required, constrained nor a term that serves the landlord (rule 55).
- subsidy-habitability-proration: Main pass closed S1's fetch request: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404 read on eCFR and saved; the row's federal sentence now rests on it and on 24 C.F.R. § 982.451 (saved by S3).
- substandard-property-receivership: Ala. Code § 6-5-156.3 quoted from a battery snippet; saved below before quotecheck.
- tenant-repair-remedies: Education only (rule 55): restates tenant rights.
- waterbed: Renter's-insurance requirement in waterbed-mn: no Alabama statute on point found in this slice's batteries; other slices may hold the renters-insurance topic.
- actual-notice-method: Rule 54: Ala. Code § 35-9A-144(c)(2) lets the landlord designate a place for receipt; the shared `notices` clause already names notice addresses, so no new clause. No agreed electronic method for statutory notices; consistent with `edu-electronic-notices-al` (no Alabama electronic-notice addendum).
- actual-notice-method: Battery engine note for the main pass: JavaScript regex has no \A anchor (\A is read as the letter A, and with the i flag the lookahead runs from every 'a', quadratic and slow enough to time out). S2 section-wide AND lines use ^ with no m flag, which anchors at the start of the unit text.
- adverse-proceeding-notice: Rule 54: the landlord-serving term (tenant forwards notices of proceedings) already carries AL as `tenant-forward-proceedings-ca` (topic key tenant-forward-proceedings); no new clause and no duplicate. The row points to it.
- adverse-proceeding-notice: Points to `edu-foreclosure-al`, written by S2 under the foreclosure topic.
- automatic-renewal: Rule 54: automatic renewal is a lawful landlord-serving option; tagged the shared `automatic-renewal-ks` (KS;SC) after reading every sentence against Alabama law, rather than writing a duplicate AL clause. {{renewal_notice_days}} is an existing variable.
- cold-weather-vacate-notice: Did not tag `cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy` (WY;SC). Its three-day absence notice is shorter than the 14-day/fifth-day term the Act authorises (Ala. Code § 35-9A-304; the main pass noted that `extended-absence-notice-ks` 'does not fit Alabama's numbers'), its entry sentence relies on lease-permitted entry during short absences although Ala. Code § 35-9A-303(b) lists when entry without consent is allowed (an absence over 14 days, Ala. Code § 35-9A-423(b)), and its carve-out for heating the landlord 'has agreed to' maintain reads narrower than the Alabama statutory duty (Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(a)(4), (a)(6)). Cautious course: education row pointing to `extended-absence-notice-al`.
- criminal-activity: Rule 54: a criminal-activity covenant serves the landlord and is lawful; tagged the shared `criminal-activity-ky` (11 states) after reading it against Ala. Code § 35-9A-421 rather than writing an AL duplicate.
- criminal-activity: Recorded Ala. Code § 6-5-156.3(h) (owner may seek the tenant's eviction in the drug-nuisance case), which `edu-drug-nuisance-al` does not mention; not an error in that row, so no finding.
- cure-and-eviction-grounds: Rule 54: no lease clause naming 'material' terms: the Act leaves materiality to the court and the AL default clause (`default-by-tenant-al`) already carries the cure tracks; a lease-set longer cure period is lawful but serves the tenant, so not offered.
- cure-and-eviction-grounds: Body says a shorter lease cure period does not work: the Act fixes a floor ('not less than seven business days'). Stated as the statute's floor, not as a waiver finding.
- double-letting: No clause: delivering possession is a statutory landlord duty with statutory tenant remedies; the AL possession-delay rows already exist (`possession-delay-al`, `edu-possession-delay-al`).
- drug-free-housing-addendum: No separate drug-free clause: `criminal-activity-ky` (tagged AL under criminal-activity) covers controlled-substance activity; `drug-free-housing-ca` is cannabis-specific to California and not tagged.
- drug-free-housing-addendum: Medical cannabis: cautious course stated (do not rely on the no-cure rule for a registered patient's state-lawful use).
- eminent-domain: Rule 54: condemnation clause is a lawful landlord-serving option; tagged the shared `eminent-domain-vi` (14 states incl. GA, SC, NC) after reading it against Ala. Code § 18-1A-29 and § 18-1A-154.
- eminent-domain: Constitution citations: Section 23 is in Article I and Section 235 in Article XII; the corpus records carry no article field, so the article numbers come from the Constitution's structure (Declaration of Rights sections 1-36; Article XII, Corporations).
- entity-landlord-court-representation: Kept VERIFIED: the statute in the corpus answers the small-claims case; the remaining doubt is court rules and case law, stated with the cautious course (Part 5 policy: doubt only case law or court rules could settle). Flagged the court-rule gap in fetch_requests.
- entity-landlord-court-representation: Fetch request closed by the main pass: court rules are outside this pass's sources (log §1), and the row already rests on the statute and gives the cautious course (use a licensed Alabama attorney for an entity's eviction case). Nothing left to check.
- eviction-hardship-stay: Points to `edu-tenant-bankruptcy-al`, written by S2 under tenant-bankruptcy.
- eviction-service-party: Rule 54: no lease-designated service agent or address clause for Alabama (TN/MP-style). The statutory mailing goes to the premises address; an appointment of an agent for service would rest on Ala. R. Civ. P. 4, unread. Cautious course: no clause.
- foreclosure: PTFA read from the govinfo U.S. Code (2024 edition) note under 12 U.S.C. § 5220 with the Pub. L. 115-174 restoration note; saved as src/USC_12_5220.txt.
- foreclosure: Redemption not described (main pass topic); row says it is covered separately.
- guarantor-renewal: Did not tag `guaranty-as`: its text states no consideration, and Ala. Code § 8-9-2 requires the memorandum to express it; wrote `guaranty-al` (same text plus a consideration recital and a sentence preserving any Ala. Code § 8-3-13 right). Taylor may prefer adding the recital to the shared clause for all states (§5a.1), which is a multi-state edit the helper does not make.
- guarantor-renewal: Deposit from a guarantor: cautious reading that Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(a) caps it; not a finding against any row.
- informal-dispute-resolution: Rule 54: non-binding pre-suit notice-and-meet term is lawful; tagged the shared `informal-dispute-resolution-or` (22 states).
- landlord-lien: No lien clause: household-goods liens are unenforceable (Ala. Code § 35-9A-425(a)); TN-style opt-in security-interest clause not offered.
- landlord-lien: Building lien (Ala. Code § 35-9-60) flagged as unsettled for dwellings; cautious course, case law not searched.
- lease-term-limitation: Homestead joinder for a lease: unsettled by statute; cautious course (both spouses sign and acknowledge a long lease of a homestead) stated.
- lockout-for-rent-delinquency: Present verdict rests on the express statutory bar; no battery run (no absence claimed). The TX-style opt-in lockout clause is not offered.
- nonpayment-notice: Points to `edu-no-notice-service-fee-al`, written by S2 under notice-service-fee.
- notice-service-fee: effective_from 2008-01-01: the knowing-use penalty took effect then (Ala. Code § 35-9A-601). rule_type PROHIBITED/RECOMMENDED: the fee is treated as a prohibited cost of collection on the cautious reading.
- owner-move-in-reservation: Rule 54: tagged the shared optional `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` (17 states) rather than writing an AL clause.
- social-security-defense: AL-S2-029 (first run) had a failed positive and a voucher line that matched every 'Section 8-' citation (920 heading-only units); not exported to the log because of its size and not cited; superseded by AL-S2-029.r2 (the page log holds both). Main pass: export it from the page if the log must be complete.
- tenancy-at-will: No battery: the topic is answered by the Act and Chapter 9, both read whole; no absence claimed beyond the stated case-law doubt.
- tenant-bankruptcy: 11 U.S.C. § 362 is 75,000 characters on govinfo; saved an extract of the subsections read (marked EXTRACT in the file), not the whole section.
- term-change-notice: No new battery: the absence of a term-change notice period rests on reading Chapter 9A whole and on the existing `edu-no-rent-increase-notice-al`.
- trespass-notice: Tagged `barred-person-covenant-ne` (NE;AZ;NC); keyed trespass-notice. Rule 54: landlord-serving covenant.
- unpaid-damages-interest: Rule 54: optional interest clause offered as an AL clause with an 8 percent hand-filled cap; did not tag `interest-on-unpaid-amounts-ca` (uncapped variable).
- unpaid-damages-interest: No battery: the interest statutes are present law; the doubt (forbearance) is case law, stated with the cautious course.
- construction-liens: Declined to tag no-liens-ar: its 'reasonable costs' sentence can shift attorney's fees or collection costs (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(3); class decision 3). Drafted no-liens-al without it, adding the consent requirement that Ala. Code § 35-11-212(a) makes significant.
- construction-liens: Questions for main pass none; the edu row's body paraphrases the statutory definition of owner or proprietor.
- electric-submetering-disclosure: Short pointer row to edu-utility-submetering-disclosure-al (rule 27).
- energy-data-release: Rule 54: tagged energy-data-release-wy (generic; already tagged for several southern states).
- energy-data-release: Alabama Personal Data Protection Act (Act 2026-552, Ala. Code Title 8 Ch. 44) is future-dated (2027-05-01); noted as a caution only, per class decision 9.
- ev-charging-end-of-tenancy: Declined tag of ev-charging-end-of-tenancy-co (REQUIRED rule type rests on Colorado statute); the end-of-lease choice is in the tagged ev-charger-installation-hi.
- ev-charging-requirements: Rule 54: tagged ev-charger-installation-hi (generic consent-based clause already tagged for TX, AZ, FL and others).
- ev-charging-requirements: Battery AL-S3-012 is shared by the three S3 EV topics.
- ev-charging-shared-area: Declined tag of ev-charging-shared-area-co: its later-tenant sentence purports to bind a non-party (not true for AL as written).
- fee-transparency: Points to fees-and-charges-vi, tagged under required-fees (its own key).
- fee-unprovided-service: Applied Ala. Code § 8-19-5(17) by its words to a paid service not furnished; whether the ADTPA reaches a given residential fee is case law (doubt stated).
- government-fee-reimbursement: After the independent check (round 1): AL not tagged on `government-fee-reimbursement-ky` (class decision 2): reimbursing a fee assessed on the landlord reads as an indemnity of the landlord's liability under law (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)). No Alabama variant offered: Alabama has almost no local rental fees (Ala. Code § 35-9A-121), and a share can be built into the stated rent.
- governmental-fines: Tagged tenant-caused-fines-oh (breach-damages form) rather than tenant-caused-fines-as (reimbursement = indemnity of the landlord's legal liability, barred by Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)).
- governmental-fines: Municipal code-lien sections (Ala. Code § 11-70B-1; Ala. Code § 11-40-62) described from battery hits only (definitions), not quoted.
- late-fee-limit: Pointer row (rule 27) to edu-no-late-fee-cap-al and late-fee-ne; no contradiction with them.
- meter-conservation-charge: Noted the PACE-type Act (Ala. Code § 11-81-240 ff., amended by Act 2026-265) as the nearest Alabama subject; it is an owner-consented property assessment, not a tenant charge.
- municipal-utility-lien: effective_from left blank: the history lines (Act 2004-522; Act 2009-570) give no effective day.
- municipal-utility-lien: Constitution local sections cited in words, not as 'Ala. Const. art.' (the citation form and quote checker have no pattern for local-amendment numbers); flagged for the main pass.
- parking-rules-notice: Found Ala. Code § 32-5-2 (traffic-control devices in quasi-public lots used by tenants); consistent with edu-towing-al, no finding.
- pet-fees: Refundable pet deposit is security under Ala. Code § 35-9A-201 (outside the cap, inside the return rules); nonrefundable pet fees and pet rent are rent under Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12).
- rent-escalation: Rule 54: tagged rent-escalation-la (fixed schedule or index). Did not also tag rent-increase-midterm-ky (open-ended landlord-set increase with tenant exit); lawful but the fixed/index form is the cleaner single option.
- rent-escalation: Class decision 11 checked: Ala. Code § 10A-20-9.05 saved and quoted.
- rent-reporting: AL-S3-017 is in the page log (superseded by AL-S3-017.r2) but was not exported: its overbroad line returned 291 units and 920 heading-only matches; not cited by any row.
- rent-tax: No lease clause: the lodgings tax is a privilege tax on the operator; how it is billed to a short-term guest is outside the URLTA (Ala. Code § 35-9A-122(4)).
- rent-tax: Fetch request closed by the main pass: the row paraphrases the sales-tax definition and keeps "rentals of real estate" in double quotes as a paraphrase; no statute wording is quoted from Ala. Code § 40-23-1, so no saved text is needed.
- utility-allowance-cap: Rule 54 option: tagged utility-allowance-cap-co (already tagged for NJ and SC under the same reasoning); its not-rent sentence is a lawful self-limit on the landlord's remedies despite Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12).
- utility-apportionment: Rule 54 option: tagged utility-billing-wv (generic, no state cite, no admin fee) rather than drafting a new AL clause; utility-allocation-fl and utility-formula-il rest on their own states' statutes.
- utility-deposit-return: A landlord-collected utility deposit is treated as security under Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(a) (it secures the tenant's lease obligations); it does not fall within the pet, premises-change or increased-liability exceptions, so it counts toward the one-month cap.
- utility-disconnection-notice-authorization: Rule 54 option: the lease may ask the tenant to authorize providers to list the landlord as a contact; tagged the existing generic clause utility-shutoff-notice-authorization-co (true for AL as written) rather than drafting a new AL clause.
- utility-disconnection-notice-authorization: PSC gas, water and wastewater chapters were parsed whole and keyword-searched, not read section by section or saved; not quoted.
- utility-interruption-submeter: No clause: Texas-style landlord interruption right has no Alabama basis and would conflict with Ala. Code § 35-9A-407 and Ala. Code § 35-9A-427.
- utility-landlord-account: No clause: landlord-held utilities are already covered by `utilities-paid-by-landlord`; the protections are statutory duties (education only, rule 55).
- utility-landlord-account: Row cites `edu-utility-submetering-disclosure-al` (S3 topic utility-submetering-disclosure) for the master-metering rule.
- utility-shutoff-statute: PSC disconnection rules (Ala. Admin. Code r. 770-X-1-.12) read whole in tab-1 (Taylor's Alabama item 11); verdict Present on an agency rule; no statute.
- utility-shutoff-statute: Tool limitation: quotecheck.py's AAC pattern (\d+-\d+-\d+) does not resolve chapter numbers with a letter such as 770-X-1, so PSC rule text is paraphrased with rule numbers rather than quoted; main pass may wish to widen the pattern.
- utility-shutoff-statute: Bucket: education only; utility shutoff rules bind the utility, and the anti-ouster rule is a statutory landlord duty (rule 55).
- utility-submetering-disclosure: Library option for billing tenants for utilities: `utility-billing-wv` is tagged under topic utility-apportionment (its key), not here; this row points to it.
- utility-submetering-disclosure: Landlord-as-utility (resale) question has no Alabama statute or rule answer; Part 5: doubt and cautious course (actual cost, no markup or fee; ask the PSC) stated, row VERIFIED.
- utility-submetering-disclosure: Battery AL-S3-004 is shared by the S3 utility-billing topics (electric-submetering-disclosure, utility-apportionment, utility-allowance-cap, utility-interruption-submeter).
- utility-transfer: Rule 54: tagged utility-transfer-nj (NJ;SC; no cutoff, no fee). Declined utility-transfer-tn (cutoff right) because a landlord-initiated cutoff of an occupied unit risks Ala. Code § 35-9A-407; utility-transfer-reimbursement-co is near-identical and need not also be tagged.
- accessory-dwelling-unit: No rule 54 option: no Alabama statute lets the lease invoke anything ADU-specific; the SFR-definition point is routed to tenant-repair-agreement-al (class decision 1) rather than a new clause.
- accessory-dwelling-unit: harvest.py blocks names an act file "Act.txt" (its regex takes the first token after ###); renamed by hand to Act_2026-240.txt, which the quote checker and verify.py expect.
- asbestos-hazards: AL-S4-002 is superseded (positive aimed at the wrong pattern index); only AL-S4-002.r2 is cited.
- association-obligations-disclosure: Recommendation to give the tenant the rules is advice in the education body, not a clause; the shared association-approval-la tag (hoa topic) covers the approval case.
- communal-land-lease-approval: No rule 54 clause: no Alabama rule lets a private landlord invoke an approval condition; a lessee of single-tax or land-trust land is bound by its own ground lease, which the library cannot draft.
- communal-land-lease-approval: Act 2026-545 effective date (October 1, 2026) read from the act text.
- communal-land-lease-approval: Fetch request closed by the main pass: the row rests on FEDERAL: 25 U.S.C. § 415(a) (approval of the Secretary of the Interior) and tells the landlord to work through the tribe and the Bureau of Indian Affairs; 25 C.F.R. Part 162 would add procedure only, which the row does not state.
- defective-drywall-disclosure: Fraud-by-suppression (Ala. Code § 6-5-102) and the licensee latent-defect rule (Ala. Code § 34-27-36(b)(3), owner exemption Ala. Code § 34-27-2(b)(1), (7)) are stated the same way in every S4 property-disclosure row.
- emergency-contact: Not tagged: `tenant-death-contact-nh` (keyed tenant-death). tenant-death is not among Alabama's 251 missing topics (it is answered by `edu-no-tenant-death-termination-al`), so a tag would answer no missing topic; left for a future Alabama tenant-death review. Resolved by the main pass; not a question to Taylor.
- farmland-hunting-rights: Tagged farmland-sporting-rights-co (keyed farmland-hunting-rights): Alabama trespass-to-hunt law lets the person in possession give permission, so the reservation serves the landlord (rule 54). The no-responsibility sentence was screened against Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4) and read as a restatement of Ala. Code § 35-15-2; if the main pass reads it as exculpatory, drop the tag and keep the education row.
- farmland-hunting-rights: Main pass confirmed the tag: Ala. Code § 35-15-2 says an owner, lessee or occupant who gives permission to hunt does not 'assume responsibility for or incur liability for any injury to person or property caused by an act of such person to whom permission has been granted', so the clause's sentence restates the law and limits no liability arising under law; property rented primarily for agricultural purposes is outside the Act anyway (Ala. Code § 35-9A-122(7)).
- foreign-ownership: No clause: the Act puts no duty on landlords and no lease term can cure an acquisition ban; a screening clause asking applicants about foreign-government status risks national-origin discrimination, so the row gives the cautious course instead.
- foreign-ownership: Ala. Code § 24-8-4 cited for national origin from the brief and edu-fair-housing-al; not re-read in this topic.
- furnishings-included: Tag only: the shared clause is keyed to this topic and is true for Alabama as written; no separate AL education row needed.
- hazardous-contamination-disclosure: New REQUIRED/CONDITIONAL clause voluntary-cleanup-notice-al: Ala. Code § 22-30E-11(b) prescribes notice words for any lease or rental agreement of an inventory-listed property cleaned to less-than-residential standards; the clause sets the words out verbatim (curly apostrophe in "state’s" as on ALISON).
- hazardous-contamination-disclosure: Fetch request closed by the main pass: the lease notice is prescribed word for word by the statute (Ala. Code § 22-30E-11(b)); an agency rule could not change the statutory text, so ch. 335-15 is not needed.
- hoa: Tagged association-approval-la (keyed hoa): its text is true for Alabama; the AL segment explains why the one-month deposit cap is not engaged by an association-held deposit and the cautious course if the landlord collects it.
- hoa: No rule 54 clause beyond the tag: the HOA Act tenant-penalty power belongs to the association, not the landlord; nothing for the lease to invoke.
- hoa: Did not write on hoa-compliance (main pass, Alabama item 7). Note for the main pass: Ala. Code § 35-20-11(b) lets an HOA board assess penalties directly against a tenant after notice and hearing, which bears on the hoa-compliance fine sentence.
- lease-copy: Tagged lease-copy-receipt-hi (keyed lease-copy): optional acknowledgment; true for Alabama as written.
- optional-lease-terms: No lease-option clause offered: whether a lease-option is a contract of sale (taking it out of the URLTA) is case law; the education row states the cautious course.
- required-disclosures: Fetch request closed by the main pass: Ala. Admin. Code ch. 790-X-1 (33 pages) and ch. 790-X-2 (14 pages) read whole in tab-1 on 2026-11-08 and searched for disclosure, own-property and lease provisions. Neither requires a licensee who leases his or her own property to state license status; ch. 790-X-1 only restates the statutory licensing exemption for owners managing or leasing their own property (Code of Ala. 1975, § 34-27-2(b)(1)). The row stands.
- sex-offender-disclosure: Coordinator lead verified: Ala. Code § 45-37-231.01 and Ala. Code § 45-52-230 read whole and saved. No new AL clause added under this topic; the widened clause is a proposed replacement for sex-offender-statement-al (keyed sex-offender-occupancy, main pass).
- usufructuary-lessor: Declined a clause disclosing the landlord's life estate: a term limiting the landlord's liability if the lease ends early risks Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4) (class decision 4); written notice plus remainder-owner joinder is advice in the education row.
- animal-care-access: Rule 54 option found: tenant-named caretaker serves the landlord; new AL clause on the MN pattern rather than tagging `animal-care-access-vi` (VI text omits the notice rule) or `animal-care-access-mn` (names a "Landlord's Right of Entry section" the AL lease does not have).
- cannabis: Tagged `cannabis-restrictions-ak` (13 states; generic text; keyed to cannabis): every bracketed option is lawful in Alabama. No cultivation-only clause added (the shared clause covers growing). `smoking-policy` left as is (main pass item 8).
- cannabis: Fair-housing doubt (whether lawful medical use is "illegal use" under Ala. Code § 24-8-3(6)) stated with cautious course; row stays VERIFIED (only case law could settle it).
- children-occupancy: No clause: Alabama requires no children term; occupancy limits are already in AL-tagged `permitted-occupants`. Lead from Act 2026-374 (lessors may use background checks to screen residents) carried to tenant-screening.
- disability-accommodation: Tagged `disability-modification-restoration-mn` (MN;SC; generic text, no state citations, keyed to this topic; restoration condition worded as in Ala. Code § 24-8-7(g)(1)). Not tagged: the ID (REQUIRED), WY, NV, OH, HI and CO clauses (state-specific citations or framing).
- disability-accommodation: Restoration deposit: Alabama § 35-9A-201(a) "changes to the premises" exception would allow extra security, but FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a) bars raising the deposit for a disability; row tells the landlord to use the federal escrow route only.
- employee-screening: Verdict Present rather than Confirmed absent: no screening duty exists, but Act 2026-374 (eff. 2026-10-01) regulates an employer's use of the state background check service, which is law on point.
- firearms: Tagged `firearm-rules-ks` (KS;SC; generic). Not tagged: `firearm-rules-la` (its vehicle sentence is a concession Alabama law does not require; harmless but not needed), state-specific CO, FL, WY, MN, HI, TN, territory rows.
- periodic-services-entry: Rule 54 option found: the (d) general notice or advance schedule serves the landlord; offered as an optional separate-document clause `scheduled-services-notice-al` (bracket: give separately, more than two days ahead). Reuses {{scheduled_services}}; no new variable.
- periodic-services-entry: No shared scheduled-services clause tagged: all put the right in the lease, which Alabama (d) does not allow.
- portable-solar: No solar clause tagged: `portable-solar-consent-wy` cites Wyoming; VA, VT, NJ, CO rows implement state rights Alabama lacks. The library's AL-tagged `no-alterations` already requires consent for attachments.
- prohibited-acts-renter: Tag only: the shared row (18 states, generic, keyed to this topic) is true for Alabama; no new AL row needed. Its "knowingly permit" is narrower than the statute's "knowingly, recklessly, or negligently permit", which binds the tenant regardless.
- protected-class-inquiry-ban: Verdict Present (Alabama speaks to inquiries by permitting some and banning discriminatory statements), not Confirmed absent; the separate inquiry/record ban is absent and the row says so.
- protected-class-inquiry-ban: Lead: Act 2026-552 (data privacy act; sensitive data includes racial or ethnic origin, religious beliefs, health) carried to tenant-records and tenant-confidential-information.
- showing-notice-waiver: Rule 54 option found: the (b)(4) general showing notice serves the landlord and is offered as an optional separate-document clause (`showing-access-notice-al`, on the `tenant-repair-agreement-al` pattern: bracket says print separately, not part of the Lease).
- showing-notice-waiver: No lease clause shortening showing notice: the (d) consent sentence does not say an advance lease consent counts; cautious course is per-showing consent (Part 5, stays VERIFIED). Shared `showing-notice-la` and `showing-notice-waiver-de` not tagged: the first shortens notice inside the Lease, the second uses Delaware 48-hour and 8 a.m.-9 p.m. terms.
- smoke-drift-waiver: No smoke-drift waiver clause offered: such a term risks Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(1) and (a)(4) (log §4 class decisions 2, 4).
- telecom-access: Verdict "Present + tagged": no state law, but the federal OTARD rule governs lease terms; tag `telecom-access-mn` (MN;SC, generic with a federal savings sentence).
- tenant-display-rights: No display clause tagged: `flag-display-oh`, `flag-display-nv`, `us-flag-display-la`, `us-flag-display-ar` carry other states' flag lists or size rules; `tenant-display-rules-co` and the ND/WY rows restrict flags, which Ala. Code § 35-1-5 may void. Edu row only.
- tenant-employee-housing: No employee-occupancy clause (the trunk has employee_housing variables): an Alabama term making occupancy conditional on employment is the very arrangement the act excludes, and the act voids the exclusion where "created to avoid" it; left to a separate employment document and stated as the cautious course.
- tenant-records: Act 2026-552 (Ala. Code Title 8 Ch. 44, eff. 2027-05-01) named as the future law per class decision 9; the row relies on the Data Breach Notification Act in force.
- tenant-right-to-organize: Present: Alabama protects organizing only through the retaliation bar; no state right of access, meeting or canvassing. Federal 24 C.F.R. Part 245 named for HUD multifamily projects. No clause: nothing a landlord may lawfully put in the lease on this subject beyond what the statute implies (rule 55).
- tenant-screening: No screening clause: the ALEA permission must precede each check and be retained; a lease clause consenting in advance to checks of current residents is not offered (the statute speaks of express permission prior to dissemination; whether a lease term counts is untested).
- tenant-screening: Findings on the two Birmingham sex-offender rows: scope, not wrong text; no proposed replacement row written because the rows are keyed to sex-offender-occupancy (another topic); the finding names the fix.
- tenant-screening: S5's findings on `sex-offender-statement-al` and `edu-birmingham-sex-offender-residence-al` were merged by the main pass into the same findings filed under sex-offender-disclosure (S4), with S5's statute quotes and battery added, so §6 lists each row once.

#### Questions to Taylor

- **deposit-escheat**: Alabama's 90-day forfeiture of unclaimed deposit refunds (Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(d)) and the unclaimed property act (Ala. Code § 35-12-71(16), § 35-12-72(a)(19)) conflict. Should Steinoak track forfeited Alabama refunds and prompt the three-year unclaimed-property report? *Recommendation:* Yes: a reminder at the three-year mark is cheap and avoids the $100-a-day penalty; the row already tells landlords to hold and report.
- **dv-confidentiality**: VAWA bars entering a covered-housing tenant's victim information "into any shared database" (34 U.S.C. § 12491(c)(4)). Steinoak stores landlord documents; does the product keep VAWA documentation out of shared or searchable stores for covered-housing properties? *Recommendation:* Treat it as a product requirement for any property flagged as federally assisted: a separate, access-limited document class with no sharing, as Minnesota's pass recommended for § 504B.206.
- **forfeiture-redemption**: Replace `holdover-rate-al` with the proposed `holdover-rate-al-v2` (excludes nonpayment terminations, adds same-day and non-stacking sentences), and move AL from `early-termination-ks` to the proposed flat-fee `early-termination-al`? *Recommendation:* Yes to both at sync; both proposals keep the clause shapes Taylor approved and remove only the parts rule 53 and the penalty doctrine put at risk.
- **carpet-cleaning**: Should the library offer an Alabama-specific carpet clause that charges professional cleaning only where the carpets are left dirtier than the tenant's Ala. Code § 35-9A-301(2) cleaning duty allows, or treat a lease cleaning charge as 'rent' under Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12)? *Recommendation:* Keep education only. Treating a lease charge as rent to reach the deposit is untested (case law not searched), and the double-deposit penalty in Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(f) makes a wrong deduction costly.
- **fire-sprinkler-duty**: Ala. Admin. Code r. 482-2-101-.02 lists Appendix M among the adopted appendices in paragraph (1) but says in (2)(k) that Appendix M is 'not adopted but are recommended'. Should the library treat the high-rise sprinkler retrofit appendix as in force? *Recommendation:* No change to the row: it tells high-rise owners to take the cautious course and ask the State Fire Marshal. If Taylor wants certainty, ask the State Fire Marshal's office in writing which paragraph controls.
- **cold-weather-vacate-notice**: Should the library offer an Alabama freeze-protection clause (keep heat on, report heating failures and leaks, tenant liable for freeze damage from its own neglect) without the WY clause's three-day absence notice and short-absence entry? *Recommendation:* Optional, low priority: Alabama winters freeze in the north of the state, and a tenant's own-neglect liability is lawful (log §4 class decision 4 allows stating the tenant's liability for its own acts). If wanted, draft it as an Alabama clause that uses `extended-absence-notice-al` for absences and Ala. Code § 35-9A-303 entry rules, rather than tagging the WY clause.
- **guarantor-renewal**: Add the consideration recital ('In consideration of Landlord's entering into this Lease with Tenant') to the shared `guaranty-as` so Alabama can be tagged instead of carrying `guaranty-al`? *Recommendation:* Yes if the other 13 tagged states raise no objection: the recital is harmless elsewhere and would let the library keep one guaranty clause; until then keep `guaranty-al`.
- **showing-notice-waiver**: Should the builder add a stand-alone separate-notice document type for Alabama (showing notice and scheduled-service notice) rather than printing these as lease sections? *Recommendation:* Yes; the main pass already recorded the layout need on `landlords-access-al`. Until then the bracket tells the landlord to print the notice separately.

### §5 Independent check

Rule 80, two rounds (Taylor's limit for this pass), one agent at a time. Each checker was a fresh agent that had not written any row, worked from the saved, hash-checked texts in `src/`, the battery log and the trunk, did not use the browser, and was told an earlier round's finding may itself be wrong. Fixes came back as exact old-to-new errata, applied by script with a once-only assertion (`tools/errata.py`); every erratum applied cleanly. The full reports and errata are kept with the pass files (`work/check/round1-report.md`, `round1-errata.jsonl`, `round2-report.md`, `round2-errata.jsonl`).

**Round 1 (all 251 answer files: 257 rows, 34 tags, 5 proposed rows, 9 findings).** 15 ERROR, 45 FIX, 85 NOTE report lines; 70 errata (14 ERROR, 56 FIX), all applied. Main corrections:
- Ala. Code § 35-9A-405(a) (rent paid into court with a counterclaim; 'If no rent remains due after application of this section, judgment shall be entered for the tenant in the action for possession.') was missing from `edu-redemption-al` and `edu-forfeiture-redemption-al` (Alabama item 9(b)); both rows corrected, and `holdover-rate-al-v2` now gives up its charge for any day covered by a payment into court.
- Two tags taken off AL (class decision 2), each because one reading makes the tenant waive or indemnify a liability the law places on the landlord (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)): `appliances-excluded-la` (its closing words limit liability only for injury and intentional or gross fault) and `government-fee-reimbursement-ky` (reimbursing a fee assessed on the owner). New Alabama clause `appliances-excluded-al` ends "it does not limit any liability Landlord has under law"; the government-fee education row now says to build any share into the stated rent.
- Absence claims widened past their batteries: five re-searched with new batteries AL-M-027 to AL-M-031 (casualty and untenantable vocabulary; advance and prepaid rent; the statute's move-out verb "vacate"; short-term, vacation and transient rentals; bank, depository and bond terms for deposits). Every claim held and now cites the new battery. Three rows whose agency rules were not searched were bounded to statutes; two rows that cited no battery were bounded to Chapter 9A or given their battery.
- `effective_from`: the helper brief had put the law's own date in this column; SOP rule 70 wants the verification date. All 262 new and proposed rows set to 2026-11-08 and the assembler now enforces it (Proposed SOP changes records the brief error).
- Smaller recurring defects fixed by errata: the month-to-month notice's rental-date rule (Ala. Code § 35-9A-441(b)); the support-duty condition of Ala. Code § 30-5-7(c)(4); "Birmingham" for "Class 1 municipality" where no section read names the city; the multi-unit limits on the vacant-building exceptions (Ala. Code § 11-67C-3(5)); who must register as a foreign entity (Ala. Code § 10A-1-7.01(c)); six federal statements resting on rules not read (rule 12), removed or made pointers; the showing notice's timing under Ala. Code § 35-9A-303(b)(4).
- Sources read and saved because round 1 asked: Ala. Code § 26-14-3, Ala. Code § 38-9-8 (mandatory reporter lists; neither names a landlord) and Act 2026-545 (effective October 1, 2026).

**Round 2 (the 69 items changed after round 1, diffed against the round-1 versions, plus the item 7 `keys` finding).** 2 ERROR, 15 FIX, 10 NOTE lines; 23 errata (all FIX), all applied. The ERRORs: (1) the delta CSV on disk was the pre-round-1 build (expected: it is rebuilt from the answer files for delivery; the final delta below is that rebuild, re-validated); (2) `edu-no-health-district-rental-rules-al`'s battery never used the topic's own term, so it was re-searched (battery AL-M-032.r2: "health district" 0 units; no statute creates health-district rental rules; claim stands, bounded to statutes). The FIXes tightened round-1 wording: § 35-9A-405(a) counts the money paid into court as well as the counterclaim; vacant-property rows now add the mandatory exemption for property advertised for sale or lease (Ala. Code § 11-67C-5(d)(3)); `appliances-excluded-al` and its education row now say whether a left-behind item is "supplied" is unsettled and give the cautious course (offer to remove each item; list only items the tenant keeps); two more statute-only absences bounded; two tag segments no longer say the clause "matches" a narrower duty. The `keys` finding was right on the law but cited a battery that could not match "re-key"; it now cites AL-S1-017.

**Edited after the last check (round 2), for Claude Code to read against the statute at sync (rule 80):** the round-2 errata and the health-district battery, plus three edits made on round-2 NOTEs (the title and notes of `edu-forfeiture-redemption-al`; subdivision numbers (b)(2), (b)(3), (b)(5) for quotations of Ala. Code § 45-37-231.01 in the sex-offender findings and rows). Rows: `edu-abandonment-and-mitigation-al`, `edu-absences-outside-title-al`, `edu-deposit-last-month-rent-al`, `edu-forfeiture-redemption-al`, `holdover-rate-al-v2` (proposed), `edu-no-health-district-rental-rules-al`, `edu-landlord-registration-al`, `edu-rental-inspection-al`, tag `rental-inspection-cooperation-mn`, `appliances-excluded-al`, `edu-no-appliances-excluded-rule-al`, `edu-no-stove-refrigerator-rule-al`, `edu-no-automatic-renewal-rule-al`, `edu-no-infirmity-termination-al`, `edu-owner-move-in-al`, `edu-term-change-notice-al`, tag `furnishings-included-la`, `edu-sex-offender-disclosure-al`, the findings on `edu-birmingham-sex-offender-residence-al` and `sex-offender-statement-al`, `sex-offender-statement-county-al` (proposed), `showing-access-notice-al`. After these edits the assembler reports 0 errors and every single-quoted statute quotation (rows, tags, proposed rows and findings) matches the saved text.

### §6 Findings on existing rows

- **`keys` (Alabama item 7).** Holds for Alabama. The rekey charge is a payment under the lease and so 'rent' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12)), which the deposit may cover as 'accrued rent' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-201(b)); it is not a cost of collection (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(3)) and limits no landlord liability (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)). Defect is notes-only: the AL segment records a search with no battery id and no rule-79 basis line. Its absence claim (no lock-change or rekey statute) is supported by battery AL-S1-017 (whole corpus, control 0, positives passed), whose security-device line includes "rekey" and "re-key" forms and found no rental rekey duty; AL-M-010.r2's lock-change line cannot match the clause's spelling "re-key" (corrected after the independent check, round 2). Proposed: add a basis line and the battery id at the next edit. Not edited (scope fence).
- **`hoa-compliance` (Alabama item 7).** Two Alabama risks the AL segment does not address. (1) Later association rules: the clause binds the tenant to association rules, including rules adopted after signing. If those are treated as the landlord's rules through the lease, Alabama enforces a rule only if 'the tenant has notice of it at the time the tenant enters into the rental agreement, or when it is adopted' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-302(b)(6)), and a later rule 'that works a substantial modification of the tenant’s use of the leased premises, it is not valid unless the tenant consents to it in writing' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-302(c)). (2) Fines: 'Any fines incurred due to Tenant's violation of association rules will be Tenant's responsibility' can be read as the tenant agreeing 'to indemnify the landlord for that liability or the costs connected therewith' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(a)(4)) where the association's fine power rests on statute (condominiums, Title 35 Ch. 8A; homeowners' associations, whose board may also penalize a member's tenant directly after notice and a hearing, Ala. Code § 35-20-11(b)(2)); knowing enforcement carries the § 35-9A-163(b) penalty. Whether an association fine is a landlord liability 'arising under law' is unsettled (case law not searched). Proposed AL variant (full fields in the proposed rows below, `hoa-compliance-al`): notice of the rules before signing, later rules binding only once the tenant has notice and, if they substantially modify use, consents in writing; the fine reimbursed as damages for the tenant's own breach, and no second charge where the association has already penalized the tenant directly. Not edited (scope fence).
- `edu-no-dv-termination-al` (dv-eviction-protection): Body says Alabama has no statute letting a victim "have an abuser removed from the lease" and notes say VAWA was "cited, not read". The lease point is narrowly true, but Ala. Code § 30-5-7 lets the court remove and exclude the abuser from the residence regardless of ownership and, after hearing, evict an abuser who owes the victim or children living there a duty of support and is the sole owner or lessee; a landlord reading the row would not know that. VAWA is now read (FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491). Proposed: keep the row, and the new AL dv- rows (`edu-dv-eviction-protection-al`, `edu-dv-lockchange-al`) carry the protection-order relief; no edit made (scope fence). 'Remove and exclude the defendant from the residence of the plaintiff, regardless of ownership of the residence.' (Ala. Code § 30-5-7(b)(7))
- `holdover-rate-al` (forfeiture-redemption): Alabama item 9(b), rule 53. The clause fails three rule-53 tests. (1) Stacking: its AL note says the willful-holdover remedy in Ala. Code § 35-9A-441(c) "is separate and not displaced by this clause", so a landlord could claim the daily charge and the statutory amount for the same days; rule 53 [NE circle-back 4] requires the stipulated charge to be an alternative to a capped statutory holdover amount, never added to it. (2) Pay-to-stay: its trigger reaches "an earlier termination ... under ... applicable law", which includes a nonpayment termination, and a tenant who appeals keeps possession by paying into court 'all rents properly payable under the terms of the lease' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-461(d)); 'all rents called for under the terms of the lease' (Ala. Code § 6-6-351(a)); because 'rent' means all payments under the rental agreement (Ala. Code § 35-9A-141(12)), the daily charge can raise the statutory price of staying. (3) Same day: nothing stops recovery of both the charge and rent for the same day while the lease is enforceable for rent (Ala. Code § 35-9A-426). Replacement wording modeled on `holdover-rate-nc` is in proposed_rows (`holdover-rate-al-v2`). Not edited (scope fence). 'if the tenant’s holdover is willful and not in good faith the landlord may also recover an amount equal to not more than three month’s periodic rent or the actual damages sustained by the landlord, whichever is greater, and reasonable attorney’s fees.' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-441(c))
- `early-termination-ks` (forfeiture-redemption): Alabama item 9(a). The AL-tagged fee is "one month's Rent ... or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater". Alabama has no statute on early-termination fees, so the test is the penalty doctrine (case law, not read; class decision 6) read against the statute's damages scheme: the landlord may 'recover appropriate damages' and must mitigate (Ala. Code § 35-9A-105(a)); an abandoned unit must be re-let with reasonable efforts and the lease 'terminates as of the date of the new tenancy' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-423(c)); after termination the landlord has a claim for 'actual damages' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-426). The 30%-of-remaining-rent limb grows with the unexpired term (3.3 months' rent with 11 months left) and has no link to the expected re-letting loss, so if a court treats the fee as stipulated damages rather than the price of an option, that limb is the part most exposed as a penalty, and enforcing a lease term known to be unlawful is penalised (Ala. Code § 35-9A-163(b) covers only the listed prohibited terms, so this is a risk of unenforceability, not of the statutory penalty). It fails the Part 5 test of working under every reading. Proposed `early-termination-al` with a flat one-month fee is in proposed_rows; AL would then be removed from `early-termination-ks` at sync (Taylor's call). 'The remedies provided by this chapter shall be so administered that an aggrieved party may recover appropriate damages. The aggrieved party has a duty to mitigate damages' (Ala. Code § 35-9A-105(a))
- `edu-eviction-process-al` (forfeiture-redemption): Alabama item 9(c). The row gives the seven-business-day notice (Ala. Code § 35-9A-421) and does not mention or point to the federal 30-day notice to vacate for covered properties (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)): a lessor of a covered dwelling 'may not require the tenant to vacate the covered dwelling unit before the date that is 30 days after the date on which the lessor provides the tenant with a notice to vacate'. The shared `edu-cares-act-notice` is tagged AL but carries no AL segment. `default-by-tenant-al` is safe (it defers to the time "specified by applicable law"), and `edu-periodic-tenancy-notice-al`'s 30-day month-to-month notice is a separate state rule. Proposed: add a one-line pointer to `edu-cares-act-notice` in `edu-eviction-process-al` at the next edit, and an AL segment on `edu-cares-act-notice` ("Alabama's notices: Ala. Code § 35-9A-421(a)-(b), seven business days; for a covered dwelling the cautious course is the 30-day federal notice to vacate (see the shared row)"). Not edited (scope fence). '(1) may not require the tenant to vacate the covered dwelling unit before the date that is 30 days after the date on which the lessor provides the tenant with a notice to vacate;' (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c))
- `addendum-precedence` (maintenance-duty-shift): The AL tag holds for precedence but not for the first sentence. "Tenant acknowledges that the legal disclosures and addenda attached to this Lease are part of this legal agreement" would fold into the Lease documents Alabama requires to be separate from it: the non-single-family repair agreement ('set forth in a separate writing signed by the parties', Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(d)(1); performance may not be a condition of the rental agreement, Ala. Code § 35-9A-204(e)) and the showing-entry general notice ('separate from the rental agreement', Ala. Code § 35-9A-303(b)(4)). If either is attached and made part of the Lease, the separate-writing condition may fail, the tenant's chore duties become lease terms, and the showing-entry right may be lost. Cautious course: proposed row `addendum-precedence-al` adds one sentence keeping those documents outside the Lease, and AL would come off `addendum-precedence` when it is adopted. Not edited (scope fence). 
- `edu-lead-hazards-al` (lead-safe-certification): Not wrong, but incomplete: the notes cite "Ala. Code § 22-37A-1 to Ala. Code § 22-37A-6" and say "State Board of Health rules not searched"; the chapter runs to Ala. Code § 22-37A-9 (penalties in § 22-37A-7, Act 2022-426), and the Board's rules (Ala. Admin. Code ch. 420-3-27 and 420-3-29) are now read: renovation in an owner's dwelling occupied by others is covered by the certification rule, which the row's body does not mention. Suggest a note-only refresh pointing to `edu-no-lead-safe-certificate-al` and `edu-no-lead-state-notices-al`. 'Individuals who perform renovation within their own residential dwellings are not bound by this chapter, unless the dwelling is occupied by a person or persons other than the owner' (Ala. Admin. Code r. 420-3-29-.02(6))
- `edu-birmingham-sex-offender-residence-al` (sex-offender-disclosure): Rests only on Ala. Code § 13A-11-204 (Class 1 municipality). It misses the Jefferson County local law, which reaches all of Jefferson County (not only Birmingham), adds the apartment-complex 100-yard rule and the same $5,000 owner penalty and lease-statement safe harbor, and the Morgan County local law with a 100-yard spacing rule, owner penalty and safe harbor. Recommend amending the body to cover Jefferson County and Morgan County, or pointing to edu-sex-offender-disclosure-al. Found independently by helper S5 (battery AL-S5-008), which reached the same reading; consolidated here. '(3) The owner or lessee of the property who knowingly, willingly, or intentionally permits a violation of subdivision (1) or subdivision (2) shall be subject to a civil penalty of five thousand dollars ($5,000) for each violation.' (Ala. Code § 45-37-231.01(b)(3)); 'No more than one adult criminal sex offender whose name appears on the Jefferson County Sheriff’s official published sex offender list may establish residence or other living accommodations in any apartment complex unless there is a distance of 100 yards or more from the residence in the apartment complex of any other adult criminal sex offender.' (Ala. Code § 45-37-231.01(b)(2))
- `sex-offender-statement-al` (sex-offender-disclosure): The use condition limits the clause to a Class 1 municipality (Birmingham), but the same safe harbor is available throughout Jefferson County and in Morgan County. Proposed replacement sex-offender-statement-county-al widens the condition; clause words unchanged. ### §7 Findings for other states

- **Ala. Code § 35-9B-2(a)(4) cross-reference (Alabama item 11; not a library row, reported for the record).** Still reads 'The individual is not a tenant, as defined in Section 35-9A-141, or a holdover tenant, as described in Section 34-9A-441.' (Ala. Code § 35-9B-2(a)(4)), history Act 2024-237. There is no Title 34 Chapter 9A in the Code (0 sections loaded under 34-9A-); § 35-9B-2 is the only Code unit citing "34-9A-441", and no 2025 or 2026 act mentions Chapter 35-9B. The intended section is plainly Ala. Code § 35-9A-441 (Periodic Tenancy; Holdover Remedies), whose (c) covers a tenant who 'remains in possession without the landlord’s consent after expiration of the term'. Library rows on the squatter affidavit (`edu-statutory-forms-al` and any row citing Ala. Code § 35-9B-2) should quote the statute as written and may note the evident error; none should cite "§ 34-9A-441" as a live section. No row edited.
- GA;SC;NC `tenant-caused-fines-as` (governmental-fines): Where a state bars lease terms indemnifying the landlord for liability arising under law (as Alabama, Ohio and other URLTA-pattern states do), the plain reimbursement wording may be such an indemnity; the Ohio pass replaced it with tenant-caused-fines-oh. Worth checking for the states that carry it, against each state's own exculpation statute.

### Proposed SOP changes

- **Brief correction (rule 27, NH 1).** The helper brief first called Ala. Code § 24-8-7 "the discrimination section"; it is "Exemptions", and the unlawful practices are Ala. Code § 24-8-4 (both read section-open 2026-11-08). Corrected in the brief at 14:30 and sent to the running helpers S2 and S3; S1 had finished. Rows citing § 24-8-7 are screened in the check.
- **Tooling fixes after S3 and S4 (2026-11-08).** (1) `harvest.py blocks` took only the first word after `### `, so an act block saved as `Act.txt`; the pattern now keeps "Act <number>" together (S4 had renamed its one act file, `Act_2026-240.txt`, by hand). (2) `verify.py` looked for Constitution files without the `CONST_` prefix; it now falls back to it. All seven Constitution files and `Act_2026-240.txt` were re-hashed in the browser and match (recorded in `src/_hashes.tsv`). (3) S3 reported that the quote checker could not read rule numbers with a letter (770-X-1); the pattern already accepted them, so S3's paraphrases stand as paraphrases.
- **Quote checker extended (2026-11-08, end of canvass).** `quotecheck.py` now also checks the findings on existing rows and for other states (finding text and statute quote); it caught seven quotations of library text written in single quotes, which were changed to double quotes. No statute quotation in a finding was wrong.
- **Brief error found by the independent check: `effective_from` (2026-11-08).** The helper brief told helpers to put the controlling law's effective date in `effective_from` (e.g. 2007-01-01) or leave it blank. SOP rule 70 says it is 'the date the row's content was verified, normally the canvass date; a law's own effective or operative date goes in the text and notes, never in this column'. All 262 new and proposed rows were set to 2026-11-08 before delivery, and the assembler now rejects any other value. Law dates remain in the notes' history lines.

None.

# Topic gaps sync (Claude Code, 2026-11-08)

**Merge.** Base 31e6155: 258 new rows and 32 shared rows tagged, matching the log; no other row changed. All 617 single-quoted Code of Alabama quotations in the delta and in log §6 and §7 (156 sections) match the live text from ALISON's code service (GraphQL `codesOfAlabama`, which answered scripted requests with an honest user agent). That covers the statute spot-check: §§ 35-9A-163, -201, -303, -405, -421, -441 and -461 are among them.

**§6 findings applied:**
- `holdover-rate-al` (Alabama item 9(b)): the proposed `holdover-rate-al-v2` wording, applied in place under the existing id, following the GA and PA precedent. It excludes nonpayment terminations, adds the same-day and no-stacking sentences, and gives up the charge for any day covered by rent paid into court.
- `early-termination-al` (item 9(a)): flat one-month fee, following the GA, SC, AZ, NC, VA and PA overrides. AL is untagged from `early-termination-ks`, and two AL rows now point to the override.
- `hoa-compliance-al` (item 7): later rules bind on notice, or on written consent where they substantially change use; association fines are damages for the tenant's own breach; no second charge after a direct penalty under § 35-20-11(b)(2). AL is untagged from `hoa-compliance`, and `edu-no-single-family-rental-limit-al` now points to the AL row.
- `addendum-precedence-al` (item 8): keeps the separate repair agreement and the showing notice outside the Lease. AL is untagged from `addendum-precedence`. The 2026-10-04 central edit (deference to a document applicable law makes controlling) is confirmed for Alabama and kept word for word. The other two central edits, `smoking-policy` and `no-alterations`, hold (log §4 item 8).
- `sex-offender-statement-al`: the use condition now covers all of Jefferson County (§ 45-37-231.01(b)(5)) and Morgan County (§ 45-52-230(e)) as well as a Class 1 municipality. The wording is the proposed `sex-offender-statement-county-al`, applied in place. `edu-birmingham-sex-offender-residence-al` now describes both county laws; Claude Code read both sections on ALISON.
- `keys` (item 7): the basis line and battery AL-S1-017 were added to the AL segment, and the citations row was updated.
- `edu-no-dv-termination-al`: protection-order removal (§ 30-5-7(b)(7)) and a pointer to `edu-dv-eviction-protection-al`; VAWA is now recorded as read.
- `edu-eviction-process-al` (item 9(c)): the CARES caveat; `edu-cares-act-notice` gains an AL segment.
- `edu-lead-hazards-al`: notes only. The chapter runs to § 22-37A-9 (Claude Code read §§ 22-37A-7 and -9 on ALISON), and the Board's rules are now read.

**Fix at sync.** Five new AL clauses (`guaranty-al`, `unpaid-amounts-interest-al`, `animal-care-access-al`, `scheduled-services-notice-al`, `showing-access-notice-al`) carried citations after CONSTRAINED_TERM or SERVES_LANDLORD, which `check-clause-basis.py` rejects. The citations were moved to each row's notes (SOP 2.04, rule 70).

- *deposit-escheat*: yes, as a product reminder. Backlog: prompt the three-year unclaimed-property report for forfeited Alabama refunds. The row already says hold and report.
- *dv-confidentiality*: yes, as a product requirement. The backlog's VAWA and Minnesota § 504B.206 document-class item now names Alabama.

- *guarantor-renewal*: `guaranty-al` stays. A consideration recital on the shared `guaranty-as` would need a rule 62 check against its 14 tagged states; backlog.
- *showing-notice-waiver*: yes. The builder backlog's separate-notice document type now names `showing-access-notice-al` and `scheduled-services-notice-al`.

**§7.** The § 35-9B-2(a)(4) pointer to the nonexistent "34-9A-441" was confirmed: ALISON returns no such section. No row cites it as live. The `tenant-caused-fines-as` finding (GA, SC, NC) goes to the backlog for those states' own exculpation statutes.
